    between Middle Eastern countries and religions.

    leaders who advocate and practice peace and tolerance,
    and adherence to civilised human values. This should
    include all of us, teachers, parents, aunties, uncles and

22   PERFORMANCE 10-YEAR SNAPSHOT OF GROWTH                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           23
      How we optimise
      our network
       One customer working with us in one
       region, or using just one of our services has
       the potential to impact sales across our
       entire international network.
       A customer in Germany exports around the world and currently
       uses Mainfreight to transport product to customers across
       Europe. Our goal – and the potential – is to be their single supply
       chain partner.

       Using a mix of modes and warehousing we configure end-to-end
       solutions based on their priorities – whether that’s transit time,
       cost, supply chain efficiencies or even carbon emissions.




                                                                             LAISA CABECABEVALU, MOOREBANK, SYDNEY, AUSTRALIA

     1   CORPORATE INFORMATION                                                                                                                        any other standards, interpretation or amendment that have been issued but are not yet effective.
     The financial statements of Mainfreight Limited (“the Parent“) and its subsidiaries (“the Group”) for the year ended 31 March 2025           (f) New Accounting Standards and Interpretations
     were authorised for issue in accordance with a resolution of the Directors.
                                                                                                                                                      IFRS 18 Presentation and Disclosure in Financial Statements
     Mainfreight Limited is a company limited by shares incorporated in New Zealand whose shares are publicly traded on the NZX
     Main Board (New Zealand Stock Exchange).                                                                                                         In May 2024, the XRB issued NZ IFRS 18, which replaces NZ IAS 1 Presentation of Financial Statements. IFRS 18 introduces new
                                                                                                                                                      requirements for presentation within the statement of profit or loss, including specified totals and subtotals. Furthermore,
                                                                                                                                                      entities are required to classify all income and expenses within the statement of profit or loss into one of five categories: operating,
                                                                                                                                                      investing, financing, income taxes and discontinued operations, whereof the first three are new.
     2   SUMMARY OF MATERIAL ACCOUNTING POLICIES

     In applying the Group’s accounting policies, management continually evaluates judgements, estimates and assumptions based
     on experience and other factors, including expectations of future events that may have an impact on the Group. All judgements,
     estimates and assumptions made are believed to be reasonable based on the most current set of circumstances available to
     management. Actual results may differ from the judgements, estimates and assumptions and the differences may be material.

   Audit Fees - Parent Company Auditors*                                                                                   3,176            3,030      The carrying amount of deferred income tax assets is reviewed at each balance sheet date and reduced to the extent that it is no

   Financial Statement Preparation                                                                                             40               45     rules have been enacted in several countries in which the Group operates, including the Netherlands, Belgium, Australia, and
                                                                                                                                                       others, effective from 1 January 2024. The Group qualifies as a MNE under these rules.

   The element of this valuation related to land has been recorded in the financial statements resulting in the revaluation of land by
   $321,768,000 (2024 $320,488,000) above cost. In addition a deferred tax liability of $50,087,000 (2024 $49,707,000) was recorded
   resulting in a revaluation reserve of $271,681,000 (2024 $270,781,000)
   The element of this valuation related to buildings has not been recorded in the financial statements. If it had been recorded a
   revaluation in buildings of $252,530,000 (2024 $240,836,000) would have occurred. In addition, a deferred tax liability of $21,207,000
   (2024 $19,495,000) would have needed to be recorded resulting in an increase in the revaluation reserve of $231,323,000 (2024
   $221,341,000).
   In determining the fair value of land, the valuers have considered relevant general and economic factors and in particular have
   investigated recent sales and leasing transactions of comparable properties that have occurred in the relevant locations within
   which the assets sit. The valuers have used two principal approaches which are a capitalisation analysis and a direct comparison
   approach. The valuations of land have been determined using the direct comparison approach to value each parcel of land and as
   a result these are considered level 2 valuations.
   Included in the Group book values above but not in the valuations are Leasehold Improvements of $91,057,000 (2024 $56,837,000).
   There were no properties held for sale included in these valuations (2024 nil).

                                                                                                                                                         (ii) Key assumptions used in value in use calculations for cash generating units (CGUs)
                                                                                                                                                         The calculation of value in use for all CGUs is most sensitive to the following assumptions; EBITDA (adjusted), discount rates and
                                                                                                                                                         growth rates used.
                                                                                                                                                         EBITDA (adjusted) is based on forecasts.
                                                                                                                                                         Discount rates reflect management’s estimate of the time value of money and the risks specific to each CGU.
                                                                                                                                                         For the purposes of impairment testing a terminal growth rate has been used for all segments based on the long-term industry
                                                                                                                                                         and country averages.
                                                                                                                                                         (iii) Sensitivity to changes in assumptions
                                                                                                                                                         With regard to the assessment of the value in use for all CGUs, management believes that no reasonably possible change in any
                                                                                                                                                         of the above key assumptions would cause the carrying values of the units to materially exceed its recoverable amount.

   A long-term revolving facility of EU€50,000,000 with ING Belgium NV/SA (ING) was established on 15 August 2017 expiring on
   4 April 2028.
   A long-term revolving facility of NZ$100,000,000 with the Bank of China (BOC) was established on 25 July 2019 expiring on 4
   April 2028.
   All facilities operate under a negative pledge and cross company guarantees.
   The facilities allow the borrowing Group to offset deposits against borrowings when calculating indebtedness for covenant
   compliance.

         1.    Net Profit (NPAT) is net profit after tax, abnormal Items and minorities but before dividends.                                                    NVOCC                    Non Vessel Operating Common Carrier

               (segments only; not Group) - see note 5 for reconciliation.                                                                                       PBT                      Profit before tax (and before abnormal items)

               - see note 5 for reconciliation                                                                                                                   Retail Freight           The industry segment that Mainfreight operates in

                                                                                                                                                                                          from source to end user
